Lesson 03: Preparing meetings and confirming action points
Original fictional case: Ten minutes remain in an office meeting. The two outstanding agenda items are printer replacement and visitor signage. Chair Farah prioritizes the printer decision. Assistant Sam can obtain two quotations by Thursday, but nobody has approved a purchase. Sam must ask Farah to confirm that action and defer signage to the next meeting. The record must not turn quote gathering into an order or claim that signage has been rejected.
Language workshop: Two quotes, not permission to buy
Communication goal: Use the final minutes of a meeting to confirm an action owner, deliverable, deadline, and deferred agenda item.
Language guidance: Sam is the action owner. The deliverable is two quotes, due Thursday. No purchase has been approved. Obtaining prices and terms prepares a later decision; it does not establish supplier selection, an order, or installation.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): We have ten minutes left. / Printer replacement and visitor signage are still on the agenda. / Which item should take priority? / We will use the remaining time for the printer item. / I can obtain two quotes by Thursday. / Sam will gather the quotations. / The deliverable is two supplier quotes. / No purchase has been approved. / Gathering quotes does not authorize me to buy. / Can you confirm that as the action point? / I will put Thursday in the action record. / Shall we carry signage forward to the next meeting? / Deferred does not mean rejected. / Signage remains on the follow-up agenda. / Sam: two quotes by Thursday; purchase not approved. / I will circulate the record with those limits clear.
Vocabulary: agenda: Ordered list of topics for a meeting. / agenda item: Individual topic listed for discussion. / chair: Person directing the meeting. / booklet imposition: Arrangement of document pages on print sheets so the folded booklet reads in the correct order. / priority item: Topic selected for attention before others. / quotation: Supplier statement of a price and relevant terms. / quote gathering: Collecting supplier offers for later comparison. / purchase approval: Permission to buy the specified item. / action point: Agreed task arising from a discussion. / action owner: Person responsible for completing the stated task. / deliverable: Specific output that a task is meant to produce. / due date: Date by which a stated task should be completed. / minutes: Formal or agreed record of a meeting's proceedings. / decision log: Record of decisions and their scope. / deferred item: Topic moved to a later discussion. / duplex printing: Printing on both sides of a physical sheet. / carry forward: Keep an unresolved item for later attention. / procurement: Process of obtaining goods or services for an organization. / supplier comparison: Review of relevant differences between supplier offers. / budget authority: Permission to commit funds within an identified scope. / motion: Formal proposal presented for a meeting's decision where that procedure applies. / consensus: Shared agreement among the relevant participants. / action readback: Spoken summary of the task and its limits. / open item: Matter not yet resolved or completed.
Speaking task: Complete the meeting-close dialogue. Read Sam's action and Farah's confirmation aloud: two quotes by Thursday, no purchase approval. Then read the separate signage deferral. Complete the transfer and check the key. Repeat the two outcomes without turning either into a spending decision.
Partner: Sam: Administrative assistant / Farah: Meeting chair
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: Complete the action summary. Preserve the quotation task, deadline, and unresolved signage item.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Sam: For signage, I'll say next meeting. I haven't got a confirmed date for that meeting to add.
Farah: That's enough. It remains an open item, with the next discussion identified but no invented date.
Sam: Then I have one task with an owner and deadline, and one item carried forward. I'll circulate that wording.
Farah: Please do. The decision log needs to show what we agreed, not turn preparation into a purchase decision.
END REFERENCE

TASK: Choose grammar that preserves the meaning
1. Use the lesson's language workshop, or one actual sentence from the dialogue, to explain a single grammar distinction in no more than 90 words. Show how it affects timing, certainty, responsibility or politeness.

5. End with a short contrast table and two supported spoken turns using supplied frames. Accept accurate alternatives in oral practice; acknowledge and replace ambiguous multiple-choice items.

Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

04Checking document versions and accessCorrect version, missing permission+

Your goal

Correct an outdated document message and request appropriate viewing access without independently widening the audience.

This lesson in the book, page 31 ↗

A Read the situation

Correct version, missing permission

Assistant Elena sent version 2 of the visitor guide. Version 3 was approved this morning, and the older guide gives the wrong reception floor. The current guide is in the shared folder, but recipient Kai cannot view it. Elena must identify the correction, direct recipients to approved version 3, and ask the document owner to resolve Kai's viewing access. The correct floor number and the identity of the owner are not supplied; neither should be invented.

Kai: Guide recipient / Elena: Administrative assistant

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. Which version is current?

Answer and explanation

D. Version 3, approved this morning

Version 3 is the approved current guide, despite the earlier email.

2. What is wrong in version 2?

Answer and explanation

B. The reception floor

The brief identifies the reception-floor information as the outdated error.

3. Who should resolve Kai's access?

Answer and explanation

D. The document owner through the appropriate process

The request belongs with the document owner, not an unauthorized expansion of permissions.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

Version 3 was approved this morning. Version 2 contains the wrong reception floor and should not remain the active guide. The brief does not supply the correct floor number, so the correction must point to the current guide rather than invent directions.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
document version
Identified revision of a file or publication.check the document version
approved version
Revision accepted for the stated use.use the approved version
superseded
Replaced by a newer applicable version.mark the guide as superseded
current guide
Document presently valid for the intended purpose.link to the current guide
shared folder
Storage location available to users with relevant permissions.locate the shared folder
document owner
Person responsible for the file and relevant access decisions.contact the document owner
viewing access
Permission to open and read the content.request viewing access
editing permission
Authority to modify the content.distinguish editing permission
access request
Request for an appropriate permission to be granted.submit an access request
permission scope
Range of people and actions covered by an access setting.preserve permission scope
mail merge
Creation of personalized documents by combining a template with selected source records.preview the mail merge
attachment
File included with a message.replace the outdated attachment
document link
Address pointing to a stored file.share the current document link
version history
Record of changes or saved revisions.consult version history
approval date
Date on which the relevant revision was accepted.verify the approval date
correction notice
Message identifying an error and its replacement.send a correction notice
merge field
Placeholder that inserts a specified source value into a personalized document.verify the merge field
access error
Message or condition preventing permitted use of a resource.report an access error
distribution list
Set of recipients for a message or document.check the distribution list
unrestricted link
Link that does not limit access to the intended named audience.avoid an unrestricted link
read-only
Allowing viewing without permission to change content.request read-only access
source location
Place where the authoritative file is stored.identify the source location
revision control
Management of document versions and their status.maintain revision control
replacement message
Updated communication that corrects an earlier one.issue a replacement message

Kai needs viewing access, not an assumed right to edit or share with everyone. Elena should ask the document owner to resolve the problem through the appropriate process. A shared folder is not necessarily open to every recipient.

C Notice the language

Own the correction and separate the access problem

An apology should point clearly to the replacement source. A broken permission is a second issue, not a reason to recirculate the inaccurate attachment or make the folder available to everyone. Describe the access needed and route the request to the owner.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Own the error
I sent version 2 in my earlier message.
Name the replacement
Version 3 was approved this morning.
Identify the affected detail
The older guide gives the wrong reception floor.
Direct recipients clearly
Please use version 3 in the shared folder.
Avoid guessed directions
I will not quote a floor number I have not verified.
Ask about access
Can you open the current guide?
Acknowledge the obstacle
You are receiving an access error.
Name the needed permission
You need viewing access to the approved guide.
Keep editing separate
Viewing access is not a request for editing rights.
Route the request
I will ask the document owner to resolve the permission.
Avoid widening access
I will not make the folder available to everyone.
Retire the old reference
The earlier attachment is superseded.
Correct the distribution
I will send the correction to the affected recipients.
Preserve the authoritative source
The current file remains in the shared folder.
Keep completion honest
Access has been requested, not confirmed.
Close with two actions
Replace the outdated reference and resolve viewing access.
6 language and precision notes
Superseded
Marks the older version as replaced rather than still equally valid.
Approved this morning
Specifies why version 3 is current.
Can you open ...?
Checks viewing access without asking for an edit.
Requested, not confirmed
Distinguishes submission from successful resolution.
Affected recipients
Identifies the relevant audience without expanding distribution.
I will not quote
Prevents guessed location information from entering the correction.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which correction is accurate?

Answer and explanation

D. Use approved version 3; version 2 has the wrong reception floor

The correction identifies the actual current version and the known error.

2. What permission does Kai need here?

Answer and explanation

C. Viewing access

The stated problem concerns opening the guide, not editing or unrestricted sharing.

3. Which action exceeds Elena's supplied authority?

Answer and explanation

A. Making the folder public

Independently widening permissions is explicitly outside the supplied task.

4. What should Elena avoid claiming?

Answer and explanation

C. Kai's access is fixed before confirmation

A request for access does not prove that access has been granted or tested.

Check explanations in the book, page 91 ↗

E Conversations

Correct version, missing permission

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Kai: Guide recipient / Elena: Administrative assistant

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • approved version
  • access error
  • correction notice
  • document owner
  • current guide
  • viewing access
  • replacement message
  • access request
  • superseded
  • permission scope
  1. Kai
    I've got your emailed visitor guide and a message about a newer one. Which should I use for the people arriving today?
  2. Elena
    Use the 1, version 3, approved this morning. I sent version 2 earlier, and its reception-floor information is wrong. I'm sorry for the confusion.
  3. Kai
    Can you tell me the correct floor now? I don't want to forward two different sets of directions.
  4. Elena
    I need to verify it in the 2. I won't guess a number; version 3 in the shared folder is the replacement source.
  5. Kai
    I tried that link. It says I don't have permission, so I can only open the old attachment.
  6. Elena
    Then you need 3 to version 3. I'll ask the document owner to resolve that separately from correcting my earlier message.
  7. Kai
    Could you just make the link open to anyone? That might be quicker than waiting for the owner.
  8. Elena
    I shouldn't widen the 4 on my own. I'll request access for you, not open the folder to an unrestricted audience.
  9. Kai
    I only need to read it. Please don't ask for editing rights on my behalf.
  10. Elena
    I'll make the 5 specific: Kai needs to view the approved visitor guide, version 3, and the current link returns an error.
  11. Kai
    While that's pending, should I leave the old attachment in the visitor email as a temporary fallback?
  12. Elena
    No. It's 6 and contains a known error. Leaving it as the active guide would keep sending people to the wrong place.
  13. Kai
    Please make the correction easy to spot. There are several messages in the thread, and a quiet edit would be missed.
  14. Elena
    I'll send a clear 7 identifying the earlier attachment, the reception-floor error, and version 3 as its replacement.
  15. Kai
    Once you've sent the owner the request, can I tell my colleague the link is fixed?
  16. Elena
    Not yet. The 8 remains unresolved until the permission and actual access are confirmed. I'll describe the request as pending.
  17. Kai
    So one action corrects the information for everyone who received it, and the other resolves my ability to open the right file.
  18. Elena
    Exactly. The 9 will correct the reference, while the access request stays visible as a separate follow-up.
  19. Kai
    Thank you. I won't forward version 2 or say I can open version 3 until it's working.
  20. Elena
    I'll keep the 10 involved and report the confirmed status. The next update won't invent a floor number or claim the access change is complete.

Answers and explanations
  1. approved version Turn 2. Approved version is version 3, accepted this morning and replacing the outdated guide Elena sent.
  2. current guide Turn 4. Current guide is the authoritative version 3, which should supply the floor rather than Elena guessing.
  3. viewing access Turn 6. Viewing access is the permission Kai needs to open the approved guide without requesting editing authority.
  4. permission scope Turn 8. Permission scope defines who can access the file, and Elena must not expand it independently.
  5. access request Turn 10. Access request should ask for Kai's viewing permission, not unnecessary editing or public-sharing rights.
  6. superseded Turn 12. Superseded means version 2 has been replaced and should not remain the active source of visitor directions.
  7. correction notice Turn 14. Correction notice explicitly identifies the outdated message and directs recipients to the approved replacement.
  8. access error Turn 16. Access error remains unresolved until the appropriate permission is established and access is confirmed.
  9. replacement message Turn 18. Replacement message corrects the earlier communication while the separate viewing-access request remains visible.
  10. document owner Turn 20. Document owner is the appropriate contact for resolving the requested viewing permission without unauthorized sharing.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Kai: I've got your emailed visitor guide and a message about a newer one. Which should I use for the people arriving today?
  2. Elena: Use the approved version, version 3, approved this morning. I sent version 2 earlier, and its reception-floor information is wrong. I'm sorry for the confusion.
  3. Kai: Can you tell me the correct floor now? I don't want to forward two different sets of directions.
  4. Elena: I need to verify it in the current guide. I won't guess a number; version 3 in the shared folder is the replacement source.
  5. Kai: I tried that link. It says I don't have permission, so I can only open the old attachment.
  6. Elena: Then you need viewing access to version 3. I'll ask the document owner to resolve that separately from correcting my earlier message.
  7. Kai: Could you just make the link open to anyone? That might be quicker than waiting for the owner.
  8. Elena: I shouldn't widen the permission scope on my own. I'll request access for you, not open the folder to an unrestricted audience.
  9. Kai: I only need to read it. Please don't ask for editing rights on my behalf.
  10. Elena: I'll make the access request specific: Kai needs to view the approved visitor guide, version 3, and the current link returns an error.
  11. Kai: While that's pending, should I leave the old attachment in the visitor email as a temporary fallback?
  12. Elena: No. It's superseded and contains a known error. Leaving it as the active guide would keep sending people to the wrong place.
  13. Kai: Please make the correction easy to spot. There are several messages in the thread, and a quiet edit would be missed.
  14. Elena: I'll send a clear correction notice identifying the earlier attachment, the reception-floor error, and version 3 as its replacement.
  15. Kai: Once you've sent the owner the request, can I tell my colleague the link is fixed?
  16. Elena: Not yet. The access error remains unresolved until the permission and actual access are confirmed. I'll describe the request as pending.
  17. Kai: So one action corrects the information for everyone who received it, and the other resolves my ability to open the right file.
  18. Elena: Exactly. The replacement message will correct the reference, while the access request stays visible as a separate follow-up.
  19. Kai: Thank you. I won't forward version 2 or say I can open version 3 until it's working.
  20. Elena: I'll keep the document owner involved and report the confirmed status. The next update won't invent a floor number or claim the access change is complete.
Dialogue in the book, page 37 ↗
Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

Correct guide, blocked link

An assistant corrects an outdated visitor-guide attachment.

  1. Assistant: I sent version two earlier. Version three was approved this morning and corrects the reception floor.
  2. Recipient: That explains the difference from the invitation. Which file should I use for tomorrow's visit?
  3. Assistant: Version three in the shared folder. I'm sorry the earlier attachment gave the wrong floor.
  4. Recipient: I've opened your new link, but I get an access-denied message rather than the guide.
  5. Assistant: I'll ask the document owner to grant the viewing access you need for that file.
  6. Recipient: Please don't make the whole folder public just for me; I only need the visitor guide.
  7. Assistant: Agreed. I've requested access to that document. The owner has now confirmed the permission change.
  8. Recipient: Let me try again. It opens, and version three says reception is on the second floor.
  9. Assistant: That's the current information. Please use that link rather than the older downloaded attachment.
  10. Recipient: I've marked the downloaded copy as old. I'll use this link tomorrow.
View access is enough for the task

An assistant clarifies a colleague's file-access request.

  1. Assistant: You asked for access to the supplier schedule. Do you need to read it or update entries?
  2. Colleague: Only read it. I'm checking the arrival times before I prepare the room list.
  3. Assistant: Then viewing access is enough. I'll ask the document owner for it.
  4. Colleague: I don't need edit rights, and I don't want to change anything accidentally while checking.
  5. Assistant: Your access permission is view-only. The owner has sent the current file; can you open it?
  6. Colleague: It opens now. I can see the Tuesday delivery but can't type in the cells.
  7. Assistant: That's expected for viewing access. If you find an error, send it to the schedule owner.
  8. Colleague: The room number on one line may be wrong. I'll ask the owner to confirm it.
  9. Assistant: Please quote the delivery reference so they can find the exact entry you're questioning.
  10. Colleague: I'll quote D43 and Tuesday's date when I ask the owner about that room.
An attachment name conceals an older revision

An assistant checks two similarly named files with a colleague.

  1. Assistant: Both attachments are called final agenda, but their revision dates differ. Which one did the chair approve?
  2. Colleague: The one revised at eleven today. The earlier file still has the old speaker order.
  3. Assistant: I'll use the timestamp and the approved shared-folder copy, not the filename final by itself.
  4. Colleague: Could you check that the opening speaker is Rosa before you send the agenda to attendees?
  5. Assistant: The current approved copy lists Rosa first and Daniel second. That matches the chair's message.
  6. Colleague: Good. Please send the link so people don't keep editing separate copies from their inboxes.
  7. Assistant: I'll do that. What should I tell someone who already downloaded the earlier attachment?
  8. Colleague: Ask them to use the current link; don't assume their downloaded copy updates automatically afterward.
  9. Assistant: The message now names the eleven o'clock revision and explains that the speaker order changed.
  10. Colleague: That's enough. Everyone can check the same current agenda before tomorrow's meeting starts.

F Say it

Two scenarios to practice

Correct version, missing permission
  1. Complete the version-and-access dialogue.
  2. Read the correction and access request aloud. Name version 3, the outdated reception-floor detail, viewing access, and the document owner.

On your own: read both roles. With a partner: swap roles after the first reading. Use the completed conversation to check any line you need to repeat.

Correct the guide and request the right access

Complete the document handoff. Identify the current version, the known error, and the appropriate permission route.

Choose one reply for each gap, then read the four turns in order. Check the explanation after each choice.

1. Assistant: "The approved guide is version ___."

Answer and explanation

B. 3

Version 3 was approved this morning and supersedes the earlier attachment.

2. Recipient: "The older version has the wrong reception ___."

Answer and explanation

C. floor

Floor is the specific outdated detail identified in the brief.

3. Assistant: "You need ___ access to the current guide."

Answer and explanation

C. viewing

Viewing allows the required reading without assuming editing or public-sharing rights.

4. Recipient: "Please ask the document ___ to resolve it."

Answer and explanation

D. owner

Owner is the appropriate role for the access request under the supplied instructions.

Read the completed exchange twice, switching roles. Keep the printed facts and professional responsibilities unchanged.

TASK: Retrieve occupational vocabulary

2. Present one role-labelled exchange with one missing word or phrase and four choices A-D. Exactly one choice must fit the stated facts and grammar. Stop and wait for my answer before revealing it.
3. Explain all four options, then invite me to read the completed exchange aloud. Offer a sentence frame for optional spoken practice, never an open-ended writing assignment.
4. Repeat for the other three terms, one question at a time. Vary the correct letter and the situation; preserve the reference facts. If a choice is ambiguous, replace the item.

Lesson 04: Checking document versions and access
Original fictional case: Assistant Elena sent version 2 of the visitor guide. Version 3 was approved this morning, and the older guide gives the wrong reception floor. The current guide is in the shared folder, but recipient Kai cannot view it. Elena must identify the correction, direct recipients to approved version 3, and ask the document owner to resolve Kai's viewing access. The correct floor number and the identity of the owner are not supplied; neither should be invented.
Language workshop: Correct version, missing permission
Communication goal: Correct an outdated document message and request appropriate viewing access without independently widening the audience.
Language guidance: Version 3 was approved this morning. Version 2 contains the wrong reception floor and should not remain the active guide. The brief does not supply the correct floor number, so the correction must point to the current guide rather than invent directions.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): I sent version 2 in my earlier message. / Version 3 was approved this morning. / The older guide gives the wrong reception floor. / Please use version 3 in the shared folder. / I will not quote a floor number I have not verified. / Can you open the current guide? / You are receiving an access error. / You need viewing access to the approved guide. / Viewing access is not a request for editing rights. / I will ask the document owner to resolve the permission. / I will not make the folder available to everyone. / The earlier attachment is superseded. / I will send the correction to the affected recipients. / The current file remains in the shared folder. / Access has been requested, not confirmed. / Replace the outdated reference and resolve viewing access.
Vocabulary: document version: Identified revision of a file or publication. / approved version: Revision accepted for the stated use. / superseded: Replaced by a newer applicable version. / current guide: Document presently valid for the intended purpose. / shared folder: Storage location available to users with relevant permissions. / document owner: Person responsible for the file and relevant access decisions. / viewing access: Permission to open and read the content. / editing permission: Authority to modify the content. / access request: Request for an appropriate permission to be granted. / permission scope: Range of people and actions covered by an access setting. / mail merge: Creation of personalized documents by combining a template with selected source records. / attachment: File included with a message. / document link: Address pointing to a stored file. / version history: Record of changes or saved revisions. / approval date: Date on which the relevant revision was accepted. / correction notice: Message identifying an error and its replacement. / merge field: Placeholder that inserts a specified source value into a personalized document. / access error: Message or condition preventing permitted use of a resource. / distribution list: Set of recipients for a message or document. / unrestricted link: Link that does not limit access to the intended named audience. / read-only: Allowing viewing without permission to change content. / source location: Place where the authoritative file is stored. / revision control: Management of document versions and their status. / replacement message: Updated communication that corrects an earlier one.
Speaking task: Complete the version-and-access dialogue. Read the correction and access request aloud. Name version 3, the outdated reception-floor detail, viewing access, and the document owner. Complete the transfer and check the key. Keep access requested separate from access confirmed.
Partner: Kai: Guide recipient / Elena: Administrative assistant
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: Complete the document handoff. Identify the current version, the known error, and the appropriate permission route.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Kai: So one action corrects the information for everyone who received it, and the other resolves my ability to open the right file.
Elena: Exactly. The replacement message will correct the reference, while the access request stays visible as a separate follow-up.
Kai: Thank you. I won't forward version 2 or say I can open version 3 until it's working.
Elena: I'll keep the document owner involved and report the confirmed status. The next update won't invent a floor number or claim the access change is complete.

Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

05Querying office supplies and delivery differencesTwelve on the note, ten in the shipment+

Your goal

Present a documented quantity discrepancy and request the missing supply information without inventing a cause or approving payment.

This lesson in the book, page 40 ↗

A Read the situation

Twelve on the note, ten in the shipment

Purchase order P44 is for twelve archive boxes. The delivery note also lists twelve, but assistant Omar has checked the shipment and counted ten. No second package or backorder is documented. Supplier contact Leila must receive a factual query asking about the outstanding two and whether another shipment is planned. Omar has no basis to allege theft, assume a split delivery, or approve payment from the information available.

Omar: Office assistant / Leila: Supplier contact

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. How many archive boxes were ordered?

Answer and explanation

A. Twelve

Purchase order P44 explicitly requests twelve archive boxes.

2. What quantity was physically checked?

Answer and explanation

B. Ten boxes

Omar checked the shipment and found ten rather than the twelve listed.

3. What is documented about a second shipment?

Answer and explanation

A. Nothing is confirmed

No second package or backorder is documented in the supplied facts.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

Twelve were ordered and twelve appear on the delivery note, but ten were checked in the shipment. The difference is two. Do not change the checked count to match the paper or claim that the missing two have already been dispatched.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
purchase order
Formal order record specifying requested goods or services; abbreviated PO.quote the purchase order
archive box
Container used to store records or documents.order archive boxes
ordered quantity
Number of units requested on the order.verify the ordered quantity
delivery note
Document listing goods associated with a delivery.compare the delivery note
checked count
Quantity established by the stated physical check.report the checked count
short delivery
Receipt of fewer units than the order or document specifies.query a short delivery
quantity discrepancy
Difference between relevant stated or counted quantities.report a quantity discrepancy
outstanding quantity
Ordered amount not yet accounted for as received.confirm the outstanding quantity
backorder
Order quantity awaiting later fulfillment.verify a documented backorder
split shipment
Order delivered in more than one consignment.confirm a split shipment
second package
Additional parcel distinct from the checked one.verify a second package
shipment plan
Stated arrangement for dispatching the remaining goods.request the shipment plan
supplier query
Request to clarify information held by the vendor.raise a supplier query
order reference
Identifier connecting an inquiry to the correct order.include the order reference
receipt record
Account of goods actually received.preserve the receipt record
dispatch record
Information documenting goods sent from the supplier.check the dispatch record
packing list
List describing the contents assigned to a package or shipment.compare the packing list
unit count
Number of individual items rather than cartons or sets.confirm the unit count
fulfillment
Completion of the relevant supply obligation.distinguish partial fulfillment
partial receipt
Receipt of only part of the ordered amount.record a partial receipt
payment approval
Authorization to pay the relevant charge.keep payment approval separate
supplier response
Information returned in answer to the query.request a supplier response
unverified explanation
Possible cause not established by the evidence.avoid an unverified explanation
delivery reconciliation
Comparison of order, documents, and received quantities.complete delivery reconciliation

The supplier should confirm the outstanding quantity and whether another shipment is planned. No documented backorder, second package, theft, or payment approval is supplied. Keep the discrepancy open until the relevant evidence resolves it.

C Notice the language

Use matching references and neutral discrepancy language

A clear supplier query separates the order, the document, and the checked physical count. Ask for confirmation of the outstanding quantity and delivery position. An unexplained difference is not proof of dishonesty, and a document alone does not establish receipt.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Identify the order
I am calling about purchase order P44.
State the ordered amount
The order is for twelve archive boxes.
State the document amount
The delivery note also lists twelve.
State the checked count
We checked the shipment and counted ten.
Calculate the difference
Two boxes are not accounted for in this receipt.
Use neutral language
There is a quantity discrepancy to resolve.
Ask about outstanding supply
Can you confirm the outstanding two boxes?
Check for another shipment
Is a further shipment planned?
Avoid assuming a backorder
We have no documented backorder.
Avoid inventing a package
No second package is recorded.
Separate document and receipt
The note lists twelve, but our checked receipt is ten.
Request the supporting record
Please check the dispatch information against P44.
Keep the cause open
We have not established why the quantities differ.
Avoid an accusation
I am reporting the count, not alleging theft.
Preserve approval limits
This query does not approve payment.
Close with the requested response
Please confirm the remaining quantity and shipment position.
6 language and precision notes
Lists versus counted
Separates what a document says from the physical check.
Not accounted for
Describes an unresolved quantity without assigning blame.
Further shipment
Asks about a possibility without claiming it exists.
Against P44
Anchors the check to the correct order.
No documented
Limits the statement to available records rather than claiming absolute impossibility.
Does not approve
Prevents a query from becoming a financial authorization.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which statement preserves all three quantities?

Answer and explanation

D. P44 orders twelve, the note lists twelve, and the checked shipment contains ten

The correct statement keeps the order, delivery document, and physical count distinct.

2. What is the outstanding difference?

Answer and explanation

B. Two boxes

Twelve ordered minus ten counted leaves two boxes unaccounted for.

3. Which question is justified?

Answer and explanation

C. Is another shipment planned for the outstanding two?

The question seeks missing supply information without inventing a cause or status.

4. Which record should remain accurate?

Answer and explanation

D. The checked receipt of ten

The receipt record must preserve the actual checked quantity despite the document discrepancy.

E Conversations

Twelve on the note, ten in the shipment

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Omar: Office assistant / Leila: Supplier contact

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • order reference
  • quantity discrepancy
  • supplier response
  • Payment approval
  • ordered quantity
  • split shipment
  • dispatch record
  • supplier query
  • checked count
  • outstanding quantity
  1. Omar
    Could you check P44, our archive-box order? The quantity on the paperwork doesn't match what we received.
  2. Leila
    Certainly. Please give me the 1 first, then the delivery-note figure and your checked count. That will help me keep the three records separate.
  3. Omar
    We ordered twelve. The note says twelve too, but I've checked the shipment and counted ten.
  4. Leila
    Then the 2 is two boxes. I will not treat the delivery-note figure as proof that twelve were physically received at your office.
  5. Omar
    There isn't a second package or backorder recorded here. Has something been sent separately?
  6. Leila
    Understood. A 3 has not been documented in the information you have. I need to check rather than tell you another package is already on its way.
  7. Omar
    Please confirm the outstanding two and whether another shipment is planned. I need both points answered.
  8. Leila
    I will check the 4 against P44 and ask about the shipment position. At this point, I do not have a confirmed dispatch date to give you.
  9. Omar
    Keep archive boxes beside P44, please. We've got several office-supply orders open.
  10. Leila
    I will retain the 5 and the archive-box description together. The query is twelve ordered, twelve on the note, ten checked, with two outstanding.
  11. Omar
    Should I leave the receiving record at ten while you check? Someone suggested matching it to the delivery note.
  12. Leila
    You should preserve the 6 of ten in this query. We need to resolve the mismatch, not make it disappear by replacing the physical count with the document figure.
  13. Omar
    I want to report the difference without blaming the driver. We don't know why it happened.
  14. Leila
    Agreed. The 7 can ask for confirmation without alleging theft or blaming a particular person. The current evidence does not establish a cause.
  15. Omar
    Could you compare the dispatch record with P44? I don't want to assume the missing two were held back.
  16. Leila
    I will request the relevant 8 check. I will not say the boxes were held back unless the information supports that explanation.
  17. Omar
    And this query isn't payment approval or permission to close the discrepancy.
  18. Leila
    Understood. 9 is not part of this exchange. The query remains open while the outstanding quantity and any further shipment are being confirmed.
  19. Omar
    Please come back with the quantity and shipment position, not just an acknowledgment that you received the query.
  20. Leila
    I will make the requested 10 specific to those questions. Until then, the record remains ten received against twelve ordered, with two still unaccounted for.

Answers and explanations
  1. ordered quantity Turn 2. Ordered quantity is twelve archive boxes, the amount specified on purchase order P44.
  2. quantity discrepancy Turn 4. Quantity discrepancy is the difference between twelve ordered and documented and ten physically checked.
  3. split shipment Turn 6. Split shipment would mean more than one consignment, but no such arrangement is documented here.
  4. outstanding quantity Turn 8. Outstanding quantity is the two ordered boxes not yet accounted for in the checked receipt.
  5. order reference Turn 10. Order reference P44 links the archive-box discrepancy to the correct supply transaction.
  6. checked count Turn 12. Checked count is ten, and it must not be changed merely to match the delivery paperwork.
  7. supplier query Turn 14. Supplier query requests clarification of the missing supply facts without accusing anyone of wrongdoing.
  8. dispatch record Turn 16. Dispatch record may clarify what was sent, but its contents have not yet been confirmed in the conversation.
  9. Payment approval Turn 18. Payment approval is separate from the supply query and has not been given by Omar.
  10. supplier response Turn 20. Supplier response should address both the outstanding two boxes and the unconfirmed further-shipment position.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Omar: Could you check P44, our archive-box order? The quantity on the paperwork doesn't match what we received.
  2. Leila: Certainly. Please give me the ordered quantity first, then the delivery-note figure and your checked count. That will help me keep the three records separate.
  3. Omar: We ordered twelve. The note says twelve too, but I've checked the shipment and counted ten.
  4. Leila: Then the quantity discrepancy is two boxes. I will not treat the delivery-note figure as proof that twelve were physically received at your office.
  5. Omar: There isn't a second package or backorder recorded here. Has something been sent separately?
  6. Leila: Understood. A split shipment has not been documented in the information you have. I need to check rather than tell you another package is already on its way.
  7. Omar: Please confirm the outstanding two and whether another shipment is planned. I need both points answered.
  8. Leila: I will check the outstanding quantity against P44 and ask about the shipment position. At this point, I do not have a confirmed dispatch date to give you.
  9. Omar: Keep archive boxes beside P44, please. We've got several office-supply orders open.
  10. Leila: I will retain the order reference and the archive-box description together. The query is twelve ordered, twelve on the note, ten checked, with two outstanding.
  11. Omar: Should I leave the receiving record at ten while you check? Someone suggested matching it to the delivery note.
  12. Leila: You should preserve the checked count of ten in this query. We need to resolve the mismatch, not make it disappear by replacing the physical count with the document figure.
  13. Omar: I want to report the difference without blaming the driver. We don't know why it happened.
  14. Leila: Agreed. The supplier query can ask for confirmation without alleging theft or blaming a particular person. The current evidence does not establish a cause.
  15. Omar: Could you compare the dispatch record with P44? I don't want to assume the missing two were held back.
  16. Leila: I will request the relevant dispatch record check. I will not say the boxes were held back unless the information supports that explanation.
  17. Omar: And this query isn't payment approval or permission to close the discrepancy.
  18. Leila: Understood. Payment approval is not part of this exchange. The query remains open while the outstanding quantity and any further shipment are being confirmed.
  19. Omar: Please come back with the quantity and shipment position, not just an acknowledgment that you received the query.
  20. Leila: I will make the requested supplier response specific to those questions. Until then, the record remains ten received against twelve ordered, with two still unaccounted for.
Dialogue in the book, page 46 ↗
Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

Twelve on the note, ten in the shipment

An assistant calls a supplier about archive boxes.

  1. Assistant: I'm calling about purchase order P44. We ordered twelve archive boxes, but the shipment contains ten.
  2. Supplier: Our delivery note lists twelve. Have you checked whether two were packed in a separate package?
  3. Assistant: We've checked the received shipment and have no second package or backorder notice recorded.
  4. Supplier: Thank you. I'll ask dispatch to check the packing record for those two boxes.
  5. Assistant: Could you confirm whether another package is due and tell us when you'll update us?
  6. Supplier: I can give you an update at three today. I can't confirm another shipment yet.
  7. Assistant: Three is helpful. We need the missing boxes for Friday, but haven't promised their arrival internally.
  8. Supplier: I'll include that need in the query without changing the delivery date before dispatch responds.
  9. Assistant: Please use P44 in your reply so we can keep it with the delivery note.
  10. Supplier: I'll quote P44 when I call you at three with dispatch's update.
An internal request has not reached the supplier

An assistant checks a colleague's supply request.

  1. Assistant: Your requisition for notebooks is in the internal approval queue. Have you received any purchasing confirmation?
  2. Colleague: No. I assumed submitting the form meant the supplier had already been asked to deliver.
  3. Assistant: Not yet. A purchase order hasn't been issued, so there isn't a confirmed supplier delivery date.
  4. Colleague: We need them Monday. Could you ask the approver to review the request today?
  5. Assistant: Yes. I'll add the Monday requirement and ask whether they can review it today.
  6. Colleague: Please tell the team we're still waiting for approval, not expecting a confirmed Monday delivery.
  7. Assistant: I will. I'll report the request status and any confirmed next step after the reviewer responds.
  8. Colleague: Could we check the supply cupboard in the meantime for enough notebooks for the first session?
  9. Assistant: There are six available. Would those cover the six attendees at Monday's opening session?
  10. Colleague: Yes. Six cover Monday's group. Please keep the purchase request active for later sessions.
A backorder is not a new order

An assistant discusses an undelivered part of an existing purchase.

  1. Assistant: Your message says the remaining four folders are on backorder. Are they still part of P58?
  2. Supplier: Yes. Eight of the twelve were delivered; four remain outstanding under that same purchase order.
  3. Assistant: Then I don't need to place a second order for four simply to keep the request active?
  4. Supplier: Correct. A duplicate order could create another purchase. These four are still due on P58.
  5. Assistant: Do you have a confirmed delivery date, or only an estimate at this stage?
  6. Supplier: Only an estimate of next Thursday. We'll confirm once the shipment is actually arranged.
  7. Assistant: Please label that as estimated in the update. Our team is deciding whether to use existing folders.
  8. Supplier: I can do that. Would you also like notice if the estimate changes before dispatch?
  9. Assistant: Please notify purchasing if the estimate changes, and copy our office inbox.
  10. Supplier: Agreed. Four remain on backorder, with Thursday as an estimate. I'll contact purchasing.

Lesson 05: Querying office supplies and delivery differences
Original fictional case: Purchase order P44 is for twelve archive boxes. The delivery note also lists twelve, but assistant Omar has checked the shipment and counted ten. No second package or backorder is documented. Supplier contact Leila must receive a factual query asking about the outstanding two and whether another shipment is planned. Omar has no basis to allege theft, assume a split delivery, or approve payment from the information available.
Language workshop: Twelve on the note, ten in the shipment
Communication goal: Present a documented quantity discrepancy and request the missing supply information without inventing a cause or approving payment.
Language guidance: Twelve were ordered and twelve appear on the delivery note, but ten were checked in the shipment. The difference is two. Do not change the checked count to match the paper or claim that the missing two have already been dispatched.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): I am calling about purchase order P44. / The order is for twelve archive boxes. / The delivery note also lists twelve. / We checked the shipment and counted ten. / Two boxes are not accounted for in this receipt. / There is a quantity discrepancy to resolve. / Can you confirm the outstanding two boxes? / Is a further shipment planned? / We have no documented backorder. / No second package is recorded. / The note lists twelve, but our checked receipt is ten. / Please check the dispatch information against P44. / We have not established why the quantities differ. / I am reporting the count, not alleging theft. / This query does not approve payment. / Please confirm the remaining quantity and shipment position.
Vocabulary: purchase order: Formal order record specifying requested goods or services; abbreviated PO. / archive box: Container used to store records or documents. / ordered quantity: Number of units requested on the order. / delivery note: Document listing goods associated with a delivery. / checked count: Quantity established by the stated physical check. / short delivery: Receipt of fewer units than the order or document specifies. / quantity discrepancy: Difference between relevant stated or counted quantities. / outstanding quantity: Ordered amount not yet accounted for as received. / backorder: Order quantity awaiting later fulfillment. / split shipment: Order delivered in more than one consignment. / second package: Additional parcel distinct from the checked one. / shipment plan: Stated arrangement for dispatching the remaining goods. / supplier query: Request to clarify information held by the vendor. / order reference: Identifier connecting an inquiry to the correct order. / receipt record: Account of goods actually received. / dispatch record: Information documenting goods sent from the supplier. / packing list: List describing the contents assigned to a package or shipment. / unit count: Number of individual items rather than cartons or sets. / fulfillment: Completion of the relevant supply obligation. / partial receipt: Receipt of only part of the ordered amount. / payment approval: Authorization to pay the relevant charge. / supplier response: Information returned in answer to the query. / unverified explanation: Possible cause not established by the evidence. / delivery reconciliation: Comparison of order, documents, and received quantities.
Speaking task: Complete the supplier-query dialogue. Read the quantities aloud: twelve ordered, twelve on the note, ten counted, two outstanding. Then ask the supplied further-shipment question. Complete the transfer, check the key, and reread the factual query without adding a cause or payment approval.
Partner: Omar: Office assistant / Leila: Supplier contact
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: Complete the supplier query using the documented order and checked receipt.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Omar: And this query isn't payment approval or permission to close the discrepancy.
Leila: Understood. Payment approval is not part of this exchange. The query remains open while the outstanding quantity and any further shipment are being confirmed.
Omar: Please come back with the quantity and shipment position, not just an acknowledgment that you received the query.
Leila: I will make the requested supplier response specific to those questions. Until then, the record remains ten received against twelve ordered, with two still unaccounted for.
END REFERENCE

Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

Communication goal: Present a documented quantity discrepancy and request the missing supply information without inventing a cause or approving payment.
Language guidance: Twelve were ordered and twelve appear on the delivery note, but ten were checked in the shipment. The difference is two. Do not change the checked count to match the paper or claim that the missing two have already been dispatched.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): I am calling about purchase order P44. / The order is for twelve archive boxes. / The delivery note also lists twelve. / We checked the shipment and counted ten. / Two boxes are not accounted for in this receipt. / There is a quantity discrepancy to resolve. / Can you confirm the outstanding two boxes? / Is a further shipment planned? / We have no documented backorder. / No second package is recorded. / The note lists twelve, but our checked receipt is ten. / Please check the dispatch information against P44. / We have not established why the quantities differ. / I am reporting the count, not alleging theft. / This query does not approve payment. / Please confirm the remaining quantity and shipment position.
Vocabulary: purchase order: Formal order record specifying requested goods or services; abbreviated PO. / archive box: Container used to store records or documents. / ordered quantity: Number of units requested on the order. / delivery note: Document listing goods associated with a delivery. / checked count: Quantity established by the stated physical check. / short delivery: Receipt of fewer units than the order or document specifies. / quantity discrepancy: Difference between relevant stated or counted quantities. / outstanding quantity: Ordered amount not yet accounted for as received. / backorder: Order quantity awaiting later fulfillment. / split shipment: Order delivered in more than one consignment. / second package: Additional parcel distinct from the checked one. / shipment plan: Stated arrangement for dispatching the remaining goods. / supplier query: Request to clarify information held by the vendor. / order reference: Identifier connecting an inquiry to the correct order. / receipt record: Account of goods actually received. / dispatch record: Information documenting goods sent from the supplier. / packing list: List describing the contents assigned to a package or shipment. / unit count: Number of individual items rather than cartons or sets. / fulfillment: Completion of the relevant supply obligation. / partial receipt: Receipt of only part of the ordered amount. / payment approval: Authorization to pay the relevant charge. / supplier response: Information returned in answer to the query. / unverified explanation: Possible cause not established by the evidence. / delivery reconciliation: Comparison of order, documents, and received quantities.
Speaking task: Complete the supplier-query dialogue. Read the quantities aloud: twelve ordered, twelve on the note, ten counted, two outstanding. Then ask the supplied further-shipment question. Complete the transfer, check the key, and reread the factual query without adding a cause or payment approval.
Partner: Omar: Office assistant / Leila: Supplier contact
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: Complete the supplier query using the documented order and checked receipt.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Omar: And this query isn't payment approval or permission to close the discrepancy.
Leila: Understood. Payment approval is not part of this exchange. The query remains open while the outstanding quantity and any further shipment are being confirmed.
Omar: Please come back with the quantity and shipment position, not just an acknowledgment that you received the query.
Leila: I will make the requested supplier response specific to those questions. Until then, the record remains ten received against twelve ordered, with two still unaccounted for.
END REFERENCE
06Welcoming visitors and checking arrangementsTwo Jordans, one visitor reference+

Your goal

Welcome a visitor, distinguish two possible hosts, and explain the waiting arrangement while preserving the actual access boundary.

This lesson in the book, page 49 ↗

A Read the situation

Two Jordans, one visitor reference

Visitor Taylor arrives for a 10:00 meeting with Jordan. Two colleagues share that first name: Jordan Chen in facilities and Jordan Patel in sales. Visitor reference V12 names Jordan Chen. Receptionist Nia must check the reference, contact the correct host, and explain that Taylor should wait at reception. Taylor is not cleared to enter other areas alone. No host arrival time or further access permission has been confirmed.

Taylor: Visitor / Nia: Receptionist

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. Which Jordan is named on V12?

Answer and explanation

C. Jordan Chen in facilities

The visitor reference explicitly identifies Jordan Chen in facilities.

2. Where should Taylor wait?

Answer and explanation

C. At reception

Taylor has not been cleared to enter other areas alone.

3. What is not confirmed?

Answer and explanation

C. When the host will reach reception

No host arrival time is provided in the supplied information.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

Reference V12 names Jordan Chen in facilities, not Jordan Patel in sales. The first name alone is insufficient because both colleagues are called Jordan. Preserve the reference and full name when contacting the host.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
reception
Area where visitors first report and are assisted.wait at reception
visitor reference
Identifier linking a visitor to an arrangement.check the visitor reference
host
Person responsible for receiving the visitor.contact the host
appointment record
Information describing a scheduled visit or meeting.check the appointment record
surname
Family name used to distinguish people with the same first name.confirm the surname
facilities
Workplace function associated with relevant building services.identify the facilities host
sales
Business function associated with selling products or services.distinguish the sales colleague
scheduled time
Time recorded for the arranged meeting.confirm the scheduled time
arrival
Visitor's appearance at the location.acknowledge the arrival
check-in
Initial process for recording or verifying a visitor's arrival.complete the check-in
waiting area
Designated place where a person remains before being received.explain the waiting area
host notification
Message informing the host that the visitor has arrived.send the host notification
access clearance
Confirmed permission to enter a defined area.verify access clearance
unaccompanied access
Permission to enter or move without an escort.distinguish unaccompanied access
escort
Authorized person accompanying a visitor where required.confirm the escort arrangement
visitor badge
Identifier issued under the relevant visitor procedure.follow the visitor-badge procedure
visitor log
Record of arrivals and relevant visit details.update the visitor log
restricted area
Space whose entry is limited by the applicable rules.identify a restricted area
identity match
Agreement between the person or name and the relevant record.verify the identity match
appointment ambiguity
Uncertainty about which arrangement or host is intended.resolve appointment ambiguity
reception wait
Period spent at reception pending the next arrangement.explain the reception wait
host availability
Whether the intended host can presently respond.check host availability
arrival estimate
Expected arrival time that has not necessarily been confirmed.qualify an arrival estimate
visitor handoff
Transfer of responsibility for receiving the visitor.coordinate the visitor handoff

The appointment is scheduled for 10:00, but the host's immediate availability has not been confirmed. Taylor should wait at reception and has no clearance to enter other areas alone. Do not invent a two-minute wait, badge requirement, or escort arrival.

C Notice the language

Warmth and verification belong in the same welcome

A friendly reception need not rely on guessing who a visitor means. Explain the small ambiguity, use the appointment reference, and say what happens next. A waiting instruction should sound like a clear arrangement, not a personal suspicion or an invented promise about the host.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Open warmly
Good morning. Who are you here to see?
Acknowledge the booking
You have a 10:00 meeting with Jordan.
Explain the ambiguity
We have two colleagues called Jordan.
Ask for the reference
May I check your visitor reference?
Read back the identifier
I have V12.
Confirm the full name
That reference names Jordan Chen.
Distinguish the department
Jordan Chen is in facilities, not sales.
State the next action
I will contact your host.
Explain the wait
Please wait here at reception while I contact Jordan.
Keep the tone helpful
I want to make sure you reach the correct person.
Avoid invented timing
I do not yet have a confirmed arrival time for the host.
Keep access explicit
You are not cleared to enter the other areas alone.
Separate appointment and entry
The meeting reference does not give unaccompanied access.
Preserve the record
I will keep V12 with your arrival message.
Check the understanding
Is the host name on that readback correct?
Close with the arrangement
Jordan Chen will be contacted; please remain at reception for the next update.
6 language and precision notes
We have two
Explains why a clarification is necessary without blaming the visitor.
That reference names
Grounds the identification in a record rather than a guess.
While I contact
Connects the wait with the next action without giving a false duration.
Not yet
States an unresolved fact without denying a possible later arrangement.
Alone
Specifies the unaccompanied-access boundary.
Please remain
Gives a courteous, concrete waiting instruction.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which host message is accurate?

Answer and explanation

D. Taylor is at reception for V12 with Jordan Chen at 10:00

The message retains the visitor, reference, host, time, and current waiting location.

2. Which sentence is courteous and accurate?

Answer and explanation

B. Please wait at reception while I contact Jordan Chen

The instruction states the supplied waiting arrangement without inventing timing or access.

3. What resolves the first-name ambiguity?

Answer and explanation

A. V12 and the full host name

The appointment reference identifies the specific Jordan intended for this visit.

4. Which fact must remain separate from the booking?

Answer and explanation

C. Permission to enter other areas alone

A meeting arrangement does not itself establish the supplied unaccompanied-access permission.

E Conversations

Two Jordans, one visitor reference

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Taylor: Visitor / Nia: Receptionist

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • visitor reference
  • appointment record
  • arrival
  • host availability
  • unaccompanied access
  • surname
  • waiting area
  • reception wait
  • host notification
  • visitor handoff
  1. Taylor
    Good morning, Taylor here for a ten o'clock meeting with Jordan. I've got the confirmation on my phone.
  2. Nia
    Welcome. May I check the 1? We have two colleagues called Jordan, so I want to contact the right person before directing you anywhere.
  3. Taylor
    It's V12. I remembered Jordan's first name but not the surname.
  4. Nia
    Thank you. The 2 names Jordan Chen in facilities. The other colleague is Jordan Patel in sales, so we have resolved that ambiguity.
  5. Taylor
    Yes, Jordan Chen is right. I might have ended up in sales if you hadn't checked.
  6. Nia
    I will send the 3 to Jordan Chen, using V12 and your name. Please wait here at reception while I make contact.
  7. Taylor
    Do you know if Jordan's already coming down? The meeting is at ten.
  8. Nia
    I have not confirmed 4 yet. I can contact Jordan, but I do not want to promise an arrival time before I have that information.
  9. Taylor
    Could I wait near the facilities office instead, so I'm easier to find?
  10. Nia
    Please remain in the 5 here for now. You have not been cleared to enter the other areas alone, and I do not want to send you beyond the current arrangement.
  11. Taylor
    Understood. I thought the meeting confirmation might be enough to go through.
  12. Nia
    Yes. The reference identifies your visit; it does not itself establish 6. I will keep the next step clear while we contact the correct host.
  13. Taylor
    Please tell Jordan I've arrived, rather than just asking whether the meeting's still on.
  14. Nia
    Certainly. The 7 message will say Taylor is at reception for the ten o'clock meeting, reference V12. It will not describe this as a new booking request.
  15. Taylor
    Reception is fine. I don't need a different room; I just need Jordan to know where I am.
  16. Nia
    I will keep the 8 as the arrangement. No alternative room or entry route has been agreed, so I will not add one to the message.
  17. Taylor
    Could you repeat the full name and department once? I'd like to use the right surname.
  18. Nia
    Jordan Chen in facilities. Confirming the 9 prevents confusion with Jordan Patel in sales. Your reference is V12, and you are waiting here at reception.
  19. Taylor
    Thank you. I'll stay here for the next update.
  20. Nia
    You are welcome. I will coordinate the 10 once the host response is available. For now, the correct host has been identified and the waiting instruction is clear.

Answers and explanations
  1. visitor reference Turn 2. Visitor reference V12 identifies the intended arrangement and resolves which Jordan is the host.
  2. appointment record Turn 4. Appointment record for V12 names Jordan Chen in facilities for the scheduled ten o'clock meeting.
  3. host notification Turn 6. Host notification tells Jordan Chen that Taylor has arrived and is waiting at reception.
  4. host availability Turn 8. Host availability is not established merely because the appointment is scheduled for ten.
  5. waiting area Turn 10. Waiting area is reception, where Taylor should remain pending the next arrangement.
  6. unaccompanied access Turn 12. Unaccompanied access is separate from the meeting reference and has not been granted to Taylor.
  7. arrival Turn 14. Arrival is the fact that Taylor is presently at reception, not merely a future appointment inquiry.
  8. reception wait Turn 16. Reception wait is the agreed current arrangement, without inventing another room or host arrival estimate.
  9. surname Turn 18. Surname Chen distinguishes the intended host from Jordan Patel in sales.
  10. visitor handoff Turn 20. Visitor handoff concerns the next confirmed reception arrangement rather than an assumed right to enter alone.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Taylor: Good morning, Taylor here for a ten o'clock meeting with Jordan. I've got the confirmation on my phone.
  2. Nia: Welcome. May I check the visitor reference? We have two colleagues called Jordan, so I want to contact the right person before directing you anywhere.
  3. Taylor: It's V12. I remembered Jordan's first name but not the surname.
  4. Nia: Thank you. The appointment record names Jordan Chen in facilities. The other colleague is Jordan Patel in sales, so we have resolved that ambiguity.
  5. Taylor: Yes, Jordan Chen is right. I might have ended up in sales if you hadn't checked.
  6. Nia: I will send the host notification to Jordan Chen, using V12 and your name. Please wait here at reception while I make contact.
  7. Taylor: Do you know if Jordan's already coming down? The meeting is at ten.
  8. Nia: I have not confirmed host availability yet. I can contact Jordan, but I do not want to promise an arrival time before I have that information.
  9. Taylor: Could I wait near the facilities office instead, so I'm easier to find?
  10. Nia: Please remain in the waiting area here for now. You have not been cleared to enter the other areas alone, and I do not want to send you beyond the current arrangement.
  11. Taylor: Understood. I thought the meeting confirmation might be enough to go through.
  12. Nia: Yes. The reference identifies your visit; it does not itself establish unaccompanied access. I will keep the next step clear while we contact the correct host.
  13. Taylor: Please tell Jordan I've arrived, rather than just asking whether the meeting's still on.
  14. Nia: Certainly. The arrival message will say Taylor is at reception for the ten o'clock meeting, reference V12. It will not describe this as a new booking request.
  15. Taylor: Reception is fine. I don't need a different room; I just need Jordan to know where I am.
  16. Nia: I will keep the reception wait as the arrangement. No alternative room or entry route has been agreed, so I will not add one to the message.
  17. Taylor: Could you repeat the full name and department once? I'd like to use the right surname.
  18. Nia: Jordan Chen in facilities. Confirming the surname prevents confusion with Jordan Patel in sales. Your reference is V12, and you are waiting here at reception.
  19. Taylor: Thank you. I'll stay here for the next update.
  20. Nia: You are welcome. I will coordinate the visitor handoff once the host response is available. For now, the correct host has been identified and the waiting instruction is clear.

Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

Two colleagues named Jordan

An assistant identifies a visitor's host.

  1. Assistant: Welcome. You mentioned Jordan; could I check the appointment reference in your invitation first?
  2. Visitor: It's V12, for ten o'clock. I don't remember whether Jordan works in facilities or sales.
  3. Assistant: V12 lists Jordan Chen in facilities. We also have a Jordan Patel, so I'm glad we checked.
  4. Visitor: Yes, the wrong Jordan wouldn't know why I brought these building plans with me.
  5. Assistant: I'll contact Jordan Chen now. Please wait here in reception while we arrange for you to be met.
  6. Visitor: Should I complete the visitor log now, or wait until Jordan confirms the meeting?
  7. Assistant: Please sign the visitor log here while I contact your host about the room.
  8. Visitor: There's Jordan on the phone. Can you ask whether the meeting is still in room four?
  9. Assistant: Jordan confirms room four and will collect you from reception in a moment.
  10. Visitor: Thank you. I'll wait by these chairs with my plans until Jordan arrives.
The visitor arrived at the old address

An assistant helps a caller at a former office location.

  1. Assistant: You said the reception desk is closed. Which street address appears on the invitation you're using?
  2. Visitor: Eighteen Harbor Street. I printed that invitation last month and came to the same entrance.
  3. Assistant: Our current meeting invitation gives twenty-four Harbor Street. Did the update reach your email?
  4. Visitor: I see the update now. I checked the time but missed the changed address.
  5. Assistant: I'm sorry the address change wasn't clearer. I'll tell your host you're coming from number eighteen.
  6. Visitor: I'm nearby but will be about ten minutes late. Could you tell the host before I walk over?
  7. Assistant: Yes. The host confirms that arrival is workable and will wait at the current reception.
  8. Visitor: Does the appointment reference remain V38, or did it change along with the address?
  9. Assistant: It remains V38. Use twenty-four Harbor Street and the main reception entrance shown in the update.
  10. Visitor: Twenty-four Harbor Street, V38. I'll be there in ten minutes.
A companion was not on the visitor list

An assistant checks an additional visitor with the host.

  1. Assistant: The appointment lists one guest under V46, but two of you have arrived this morning.
  2. Visitor: My colleague came with me to discuss the samples. I forgot to mention them to the host.
  3. Assistant: I'll check with the host before arranging your colleague's access beyond reception.
  4. Visitor: Can we both wait in reception while you check with the host?
  5. Assistant: Certainly. May I tell the host your colleague's name and that they're joining the sample discussion?
  6. Visitor: Yes, it's Robin Ellis. There are no changes to the samples or the meeting purpose.
  7. Assistant: The host has confirmed Robin can join. We'll complete the separate visitor details for each of you.
  8. Visitor: Thank you. Should Robin use my badge, or will there be a separate one issued?
  9. Assistant: Robin needs a separate visitor badge after checking in; please keep your own.
  10. Visitor: Understood. We'll complete our own details and wait here for the host to collect us.
